<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_(02).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg/220px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg/330px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg/440px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_%2802%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4752" data-file-height="3168" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Budynek_Ministerstwa_Spraw_Zagranicznych_Republiki_G%C3%B3rskiego_Karabachu_(02).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Artsakh in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Ministry of Foreign Affairs is based in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>. Since no UN member or observer currently recognises Artsakh, none of its foreign relations are of an official diplomatic nature. However, the Republic of Artsakh operates five permanent Missions and one Bureau of Social-Politic Information in France. Artsakh's Permanent Missions exist in <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a>, Australia, France, Germany, Russia, the United States, and one for Middle East countries based in <a href="/wiki/Beirut" title="Beirut">Beirut</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> The goals of the offices are to present the Republic's positions on various issues, to provide information and to facilitate the peace process.
</p><p>In his 2015 speech, the President of Armenia <a href="/wiki/Serzh_Sargsyan" title="Serzh Sargsyan">Serzh Sargsyan</a> stated that he considered Nagorno-Karabakh "an inseparable part of Armenia".<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Republic of Artsakh is neither a member nor observer of the UN or any of its specialised agencies. However, it is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Community_for_Democracy_and_Rights_of_Nations" title="Community for Democracy and Rights of Nations">Community for Democracy and Rights of Nations</a>, commonly known as the "Commonwealth of Unrecognized States", and is recognised by <a href="/wiki/Transnistria" title="Transnistria">Transnistria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia" title="Abkhazia">Abkhazia</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Ossetia" title="South Ossetia">South Ossetia</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Defence_Army" title="Artsakh Defence Army">Artsakh Defence Army</a> and <a href="/wiki/First_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" title="First Nagorno-Karabakh War">First Nagorno-Karabakh War</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG/220px-Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG/330px-Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG/440px-Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_Graveyard.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The graves of Armenian soldiers in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>According to the Constitution of Artsakh, the army is under the civilian command of the government.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Defense_Army" class="mw-redirect" title="Artsakh Defense Army">Artsakh Defense Army</a> was officially established on 9 May 1992 as a defence against Azerbaijan. It fought the Azerbaijani army to a ceasefire on 12 May 1994.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Currently the Artsakh Defense Army consists of around 18,000–20,000 officers and soldiers. However, only 8,500 citizens from Artsakh serve in the Artsakh army; some 10,000 come from Armenia. There are also 177–316 <a href="/wiki/Tank" title="Tank">tanks</a>, 256–324 additional fighting vehicles, and 291–322 guns and <a href="/wiki/Mortar_(weapon)" title="Mortar (weapon)">mortars</a>. Armenia supplies arms and other military necessities to Artsakh. Several battalions of Armenia's army are deployed directly in the Artsakh zone on occupied Azerbaijani territory.<sup id="cite_ref-Blandy_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Blandy-115">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Artsakh Defense Army <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Shusha" class="mw-redirect" title="Capture of Shusha">fought in Shusha</a> in 1992, opening the <a href="/wiki/Lachin_corridor" title="Lachin corridor">Lachin corridor</a> between Armenia and Nagorno-Karabakh (1992), and staged the defence of the <a href="/wiki/Martakert" title="Martakert">Martakert</a> front from 1992 to 1994.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Current_situation">Current situation</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Political_status_of_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Political status of Nagorno-Karabakh">Political status of Nagorno-Karabakh</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Ambox_current_red.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">November 2020</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Displaced_people">Displaced people</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Refugees_in_Azerbaijan" title="Refugees in Azerbaijan">Refugees in Azerbaijan</a></div>
<p>The first Nagorno-Karabakh conflict has resulted in the displacement of 597,000 Azerbaijanis (this figure includes 230,000 children born to <a href="/wiki/Internally_displaced_person" title="Internally displaced person">internally displaced people</a> (IDPs) and 54,000 who have returned)<sup id="cite_ref-auto_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-116">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> including Artsakh, and 220,000 Azeris, 18,000 Kurds and 3,500 Russians fled from Armenia to Azerbaijan from 1988 to 1989. The Azerbaijani government has estimated that 63% of IDPs lived below the poverty line as compared to 49% of the total population. About 154,000 lived in the capital, Baku. According to the <a href="/wiki/International_Organization_for_Migration" title="International Organization for Migration">International Organization for Migration</a>, 40,000 IDPs lived in camps, 60,000 in dugout shelters, and 20,000 in railway cars. Forty-thousand IDPs lived in EU-funded settlements and UNHCR provided housing for another 40,000. Another 5,000 IDPs lived in abandoned or rapidly deteriorating schools. Others lived in trains, on roadsides in half-constructed buildings, or in public buildings such as tourist and health facilities. Tens of thousands lived in seven tent camps where poor water supply and sanitation caused <a href="/wiki/Gastrointestinal_infection" class="mw-redirect" title="Gastrointestinal infection">gastrointestinal infections</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">malaria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government required IDPs to register their place of residence in an attempt to better target the limited and largely inadequate national and international assistance due to the Armenian advocated and US imposed restrictions on humanitarian aid to Azerbaijan. Many IDPs were from rural areas and found it difficult to integrate into the urban labour market. Many international humanitarian agencies reduced or ceased assistance for IDPs citing increasing oil revenues of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-wrs2005_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wrs2005-119">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> The infant mortality among displaced Azerbaijani children is 3–4 times higher than in the rest of the population. The rate of <a href="/wiki/Stillbirth" title="Stillbirth">stillbirth</a> was 88.2 per 1,000 births among the internally displaced people. The majority of the displaced have lived in difficult conditions for more than 13 years.<sup id="cite_ref-gidp_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gidp-120">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the <a href="/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_war" class="mw-redirect" title="2020 Nagorno-Karabakh war">2020 war</a> <a href="/wiki/Ilham_Aliyev" title="Ilham Aliyev">President Aliyev</a> stated he intends for refugees to return to the area.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> While many former cities are currently uninhabitable,<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> the Azerbaijani government and some Azerbaijani companies have announced plans to rebuild infrastructure and invest in the newly controlled territories.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The Azerbaijani military is clearing mines prior to resettlement, which may take 10–13 years.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>280,000 people—virtually all ethnic Armenians who fled Azerbaijan during the 1988–1993 war over the disputed region of Artsakh—were living in refugee-like circumstances in Armenia.<sup id="cite_ref-wrs2001_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wrs2001-127">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Some left the country, principally to Russia. Their children born in Armenia acquire citizenship automatically. Their numbers are thus subject to constant decline due to departure, and de-registration required for naturalisation. Of these, about 250,000 fled Azerbaijan (areas outside Nagorno-Karabakh); approximately 30,000 came from Nagorno-Karabakh. All were registered with the government as refugees at year's end.<sup id="cite_ref-wrs2001_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wrs2001-127">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Land_mines">Land mines</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Land_mine_situation_in_Nagorno-Karabakh" class="mw-redirect" title="Land mine situation in Nagorno-Karabakh">Land mine situation in Nagorno-Karabakh</a></div>
<p>Mines were laid in the region from 1991 to 1994 by both conflicting parties in the <a href="/wiki/First_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" title="First Nagorno-Karabakh War">first Nagorno-Karabakh War</a>. The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Development Program">United Nations Development Program</a> (UNDP) claims that 123 people have been killed and over 300 injured by landmines near the disputed enclave of Nagorno-Karabakh since a 1994 truce ended a six-year conflict between ethnic Armenian and Azerbaijani forces.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/HALO_Trust" title="HALO Trust">HALO Trust</a>, a UK-based demining NGO, is the only international organisation conducting <a href="/wiki/Demining" title="Demining">demining</a> in Nagorno Karabakh.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> They have destroyed 180,858 small arms ammunition, 48,572 units of "other explosive items", 12,423 cluster bombs, 8,733 anti-personnel landmines, and 2,584 anti-tank landmines between 2000 and 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> By 2018, they had cleared 88% of the territory's minefields, with a target to clear the rest by 2020. The main cities of <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a> and <a href="/wiki/Shusha" title="Shusha">Shusha</a>, as well as the main north–south highway, have been cleared and are safe for travel. The demining effort has been largely funded by the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development" title="United States Agency for International Development">United States Agency for International Development</a> (USAID).<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png/220px-Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="199" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png/330px-Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png/440px-Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png 2x" data-file-width="2999" data-file-height="2715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Topo_map_Artsakh_en.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Topographic map of Artsakh in the borders 1994−2020</div></div></div>
<p>The Artsakh Republic is mountainous, a feature which has given it its former name (from the Russian for "Mountainous/Highland Karabakh"). It is 3,170&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1,224&#160;sq&#160;mi) in area.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The largest water body is the <a href="/wiki/Sarsang_Reservoir" title="Sarsang Reservoir">Sarsang Reservoir</a>, and the major rivers are the <a href="/wiki/Tartar_(river)" title="Tartar (river)">Tartar</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Khachen_(river)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Khachen (river) (page does not exist)">Khachen</a> rivers.<sup id="cite_ref-Overview_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Overview-133">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The country is on a plateau which slopes downwards towards the east and southeast, with the average altitude being 1,100&#160;m (3,600&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above sea level</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Most rivers in the country flow towards the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Artsakh_Valley&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Artsakh Valley (page does not exist)">Artsakh Valley</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_134-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The climate is mild and temperate. The average temperature is 11&#160;°C (52&#160;°F), which fluctuates annually between 22&#160;°C (72&#160;°F) in July and −1&#160;°C (30&#160;°F) in January. The average precipitation can reach 710&#160;mm (28&#160;in) in some regions, and it is foggy for over 100 days a year.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_134-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Over 2,000 kinds of plants exist in Artsakh, and more than 36% of the country is forested. The plant life on the <a href="/wiki/Steppes" class="mw-redirect" title="Steppes">steppes</a> consists mostly of semi-desert vegetation, while <a href="/wiki/Subalpine_zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Subalpine zone">subalpine zone</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alpine_tundra" title="Alpine tundra">alpine tundra</a> ecosystems can be found above the forest in the highlands and mountains.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_134-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh" title="Administrative divisions of the Republic of Artsakh">Administrative divisions of the Republic of Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Street_in_Martuni.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Street_in_Martuni.jpg/220px-Street_in_Martuni.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Street_in_Martuni.jpg/330px-Street_in_Martuni.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Street_in_Martuni.jpg/440px-Street_in_Martuni.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Street_in_Martuni.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Martuni,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Martuni, Nagorno-Karabakh">Martuni</a> town</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA,_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG/220px-%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG/330px-%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG/440px-%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA%2C_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BA,_%D0%BD%D0%BE%D1%8F%D0%B1%D1%80%D1%8C_2009%D0%B3..JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mountain view in Martakert region</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg/220px-Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="287" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg/330px-Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg/440px-Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="825" data-file-height="1075" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Regions of Artsakh:<br />1:&#160;Martakert; 2:&#160;Askeran; 3:&#160;Stepanakert (city); 4:&#160;Martuni; 5:&#160;Shushi<br />Claimed regions:<br />6:&#160;Hadrut; 7:&#160;Shahumyan<br />(Horizontal dashed lines indicate territory under the control of Azerbaijan.)</div></div></div>
<p>Artsakh is divided into <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh" title="Administrative divisions of the Republic of Artsakh">seven provinces</a> and one special <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">administrative city</a>. According to the authorities of Artsakh, it consists of the territories in which the Nagorno-Karabakh Republic was <a href="/wiki/1991_Nagorno-Karabakh_independence_referendum" title="1991 Nagorno-Karabakh independence referendum">proclaimed</a>: the former <a href="/wiki/Nagorno-Karabakh_Autonomous_Oblast" title="Nagorno-Karabakh Autonomous Oblast">Nagorno-Karabakh Autonomous Oblast</a> (NKAO), the <a href="/wiki/Shahumyan_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Shahumyan Region">Shahumyan Region</a> and the Getashen subdistrict; and those territories that <a href="/wiki/Armenian-occupied_territories_surrounding_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Armenian-occupied territories surrounding Nagorno-Karabakh">formed part</a> of the Republic of Artsakh before the <a href="/wiki/Second_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" title="Second Nagorno-Karabakh War">Second Nagorno-Karabakh War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-armradio_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-armradio-135">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-newsarmenia_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-newsarmenia-136">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-deoccupation_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-deoccupation-137">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2022, the territory of Artsakh includes most of four districts of the former Nagorno-Karabakh Autonomous Oblast, with the fifth district, <a href="/wiki/Hadrut_Province" title="Hadrut Province">Hadrut Province</a>, completely under Azerbaijani control. Also claimed by Artsakh is the <a href="/wiki/Shahumyan_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Shahumyan Region">Shahumyan Region</a> of the Azerbaijan SSR, which has been under Azerbaijani control since the <a href="/wiki/First_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" title="First Nagorno-Karabakh War">First Nagorno-Karabakh War</a>. While the Shahumyan Region was not part of the Nagorno-Karabakh Autonomous Oblast, representatives from Shahumyan declared independence along with the Oblast, and the proclamation of Artsakh includes the Shahumyan region within its borders.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the end of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" title="Second Nagorno-Karabakh War">Second Nagorno-Karabakh War</a>, an <a href="/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_ceasefire_agreement" title="2020 Nagorno-Karabakh ceasefire agreement">agreement</a> was signed according to which most of the controlled territories of the Republic of Artsakh were transferred to Azerbaijani control, but the Republic of Artsakh continues to claim these territories.<sup id="cite_ref-armradio_135-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-armradio-135">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-newsarmenia_136-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-newsarmenia-136">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-deoccupation_137-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-deoccupation-137">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the Republic of Artsakh's declaration of independence, the Azerbaijani government abolished the NKAO and created Azerbaijani districts in its place. As a result, some of Artsakh's divisions corresponded with the <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Azerbaijan" title="Administrative divisions of Azerbaijan">Azerbaijani districts</a>, while others had different borders.<sup id="cite_ref-azerbregions_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-azerbregions-139">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh" title="Demographics of the Republic of Artsakh">Demographics of the Republic of Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg/220px-Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg/330px-Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg/440px-Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tumo_Stepanakert.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Children at <a href="/wiki/Tumo_Center_for_Creative_Technologies" title="Tumo Center for Creative Technologies">Tumo Center</a> Artsakh branch</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Azatamartikneri_boulevard,_Stepanakert.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg/220px-Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg/330px-Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg/440px-Azatamartikneri_boulevard%2C_Stepanakert.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="682" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Azatamartikneri_boulevard,_Stepanakert.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Freedom Fighters' Boulevard in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 2002, the country's population was 145,000, made up of 95% Armenians and 5% others.<sup id="cite_ref-Overview_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Overview-133">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> This composition represents a sharp change from the 1979 and 1989 census, when the Azerbaijani population was 23 and 21.5 per cent, respectively. The annual birth rate was recorded at 2,200–2,300 per year, an increase from nearly 1,500 in 1999.
</p><p>OSCE report, released in March 2011, estimates the population of the "seven occupied territories surrounding Nagorno-Karabakh" to be 14,000, and states "there has been no significant growth in the population since 2005."<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/International_Crisis_Group" title="International Crisis Group">International Crisis Group</a> report published in December 2019 recorded the population of these territories to be 17,000, or 11.48% of the total population: 15,000 west and southwest of the former oblast, and 2000 in the <a href="/wiki/Agdam_District" class="mw-redirect" title="Agdam District">Agdam District</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Until 2000, the country's net migration was at a negative.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> For the first half of 2007, 1,010 births and 659 deaths were reported, with a net emigration of 27.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to age group: 15,700 (0–6), 25,200 (7–17) 75,800 (18–59) and 21,000 (60+)
</p><p>Population by province (2006):
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a> 54,500 (2013)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Martuni_Province" title="Martuni Province">Martuni</a> 23,200</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Martakert" title="Martakert">Martakert</a> 19,000</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Askeran_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Askeran Region">Askeran</a> 17,400 (2007)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Hadrut_Region" class="mw-redirect" title="Hadrut Region">Hadrut</a> 12,300 (2009)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Qashatagh_Province" class="mw-redirect" title="Qashatagh Province">Kashatagh</a> 9,800</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shushi" class="mw-redirect" title="Shushi">Shushi</a> 5,000 (2009)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shahumyan" class="mw-redirect" title="Shahumyan">Shahumyan</a> 2,800</li></ul>
