<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Armenian_dialects" title="Armenian dialects">Armenian dialects</a> and <a href="/wiki/Russian_dialects" title="Russian dialects">Russian dialects</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a> functions as the only state language and an official language in the Republic of Artsakh,<sup id="cite_ref-ap2-2021_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ap2-2021-151">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> and is the native language of over 99% of the population. Under Soviet rule, the ethnic Armenian population of Nagorno-Karabkah became more proficient in Russian than both Azerbaijanis in the region, and Armenians in the Armenian SSR.Few Armenians learnt Azerbaijani, and the language was actively removed following the Nagorno-Karabakh War. While Russian remained in some use after this time, and was valued as a second language, it was not widely spoken at a native level.<sup id="cite_ref-Muth2014_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Muth2014-25">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Russian_language" title="Russian language">Russian</a> is widely spoken in Arstakh, with efforts having been made since late 2020 in the parliament in Stepanakert to establish it as an additional official language; the official justifications for this being that Russian was already the second language of many residents, and that it would create "conditions for deepening cooperation in all spheres, [as well as contributing] to the development of relations within the legal framework."<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Artsakh)" title="National Assembly (Artsakh)">Parliament of Artsakh</a> approved a bill grating Russian official status on March 25, 2021, with 27 votes in favour, 0 votes against and 2 abstentions.<sup id="cite_ref-ap2-2021_151-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ap2-2021-151">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cities_and_towns">Cities and towns</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Artsakh" title="List of cities and towns in Artsakh">List of cities and towns in Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png/220px-Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="294" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png/330px-Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png/440px-Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png 2x" data-file-width="906" data-file-height="1209" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Republic_of_Artsakh_map.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Main cities and towns in Artsakh (territorial control shown is prior to the 2020 war)</div></div></div>
<p>The capital and by far largest city in Artsakh is Stepanakert (55,200 inhabitants in 2015), followed by <a href="/wiki/Martuni,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Martuni, Nagorno-Karabakh">Martuni</a> (5,700), <a href="/wiki/Martakert" title="Martakert">Martakert</a> (4,600), <a href="/wiki/Chartar" title="Chartar">Chartar</a> (4,000), <a href="/wiki/Askeran" title="Askeran">Askeran</a> (2,300), <a href="/wiki/Haterk" title="Haterk">Haterk</a> (c. 1,600), <a href="/wiki/Berdashen,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Berdashen, Nagorno-Karabakh">Berdashen</a> (c. 1,600), <a href="/wiki/Vank,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Vank, Nagorno-Karabakh">Vank</a> (c. 1,600), <a href="/wiki/Noragyugh,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Noragyugh, Nagorno-Karabakh">Noragyugh</a> (c. 1,500), <a href="/wiki/Khojaly_(town)" title="Khojaly (town)">Ivanyan</a> (c. 1,400), <a href="/wiki/Taghavard" title="Taghavard">Taghavard</a> (c. 1,300), <a href="/wiki/Gishi,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Gishi, Nagorno-Karabakh">Gishi</a> (c. 1,100), <a href="/wiki/Karmir_Shuka" title="Karmir Shuka">Karmir Shuka</a> (c. 1,100), <a href="/wiki/Sos,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Sos, Nagorno-Karabakh">Sos</a> (c. 1,100), <a href="/wiki/Aygestan,_Askeran" title="Aygestan, Askeran">Aygestan</a> (c. 1,100) and <a href="/wiki/Khnapat" title="Khnapat">Khnapat</a> (c. 1,000).<sup id="cite_ref-Census2015_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2015-153">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2015statistics_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2015statistics-154">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> This list only includes towns that remain in Artsakh after the 2020 war.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Resettlement_attempts_after_1994">Resettlement attempts after 1994</span></h3>
<p>From 1989 to 1994, there was significant depopulation in the territory that ended up held by Artsakh, leaving only around 40% of the pre-war population. Much of this was due to the displacement and death of Azerbaijani residents in both the former NKOA and the surrounding territories, leaving some former urban areas virtually empty. The Russian minority present also declined, meaning the resulting population was almost 100% Armenian. Beginning in 1995, the population began to increase due to births and immigration.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While the territory captured outside the former NKAO was initially treated as a potential bargaining chip, it slowly began to be seen as part of the country by both officials and the general population.<sup id="cite_ref-Toal_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Toal-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>-based administration launched various programs aimed at bringing in permanent Armenian settlers to the depopulated lands, including into regions previously populated by Azeris, with those that bordered Armenia – Lachin and Kalbajar – being the priority.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> Lachin was key to a land connection between Armenia and the former NKAO, and Kalbajar had water resources utilised by both Artsakh and Armenia.<sup id="cite_ref-Toal_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Toal-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Azerbaijan regards this as a violation of Article 49 of the <a href="/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention" title="Fourth Geneva Convention">Fourth Geneva Convention</a>, to which Armenia became party in 1993, whereby "[t]he Occupying Power shall not deport or transfer parts of its own civilian population into the territory it occupies".<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> The ruling party of Azerbaijan accuses the Armenian side of artificially changing the demographic situation and the ethnic composition of the occupied region so that it can lay future claims to them, comparing this to the 1950s campaign of resettling diaspora Armenians in previously Azeri-populated locales in Soviet Armenia where Azeris were forcibly <a href="/wiki/Deportation_of_Azerbaijanis_from_Armenia" title="Deportation of Azerbaijanis from Armenia">deported from in 1948–1950</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1979, the total Armenian population of the districts of Kalbajar, Lachin, Qubadli, Zangilan, Jabrayil, Fuzuli and Agdam was around 1,400 people.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> An OSCE fact-finding mission established at Azerbaijan's request visited these regions in February 2005 with the intention to assess the scale of the settlement attempts. The mission's findings showed that these districts had as of 2005 an overall population of 14,000 people, mostly living in precarious social conditions. It consisted primarily of ethnic Armenians displaced from the non-conflict zones of Azerbaijan during the war. It was noted, however, that most of them had settled in the conflict zone after having lived in Armenia for several years and some held Armenian passports and even voted in Armenian elections. A smaller segment of the settlers was originally from the towns of <a href="/wiki/Gyumri" title="Gyumri">Gyumri</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spitak" title="Spitak">Spitak</a> in Armenia who had lived in temporary shelters following the devastating <a href="/wiki/1988_Armenian_earthquake" title="1988 Armenian earthquake">1988 earthquake</a> before moving to Karabakh, as well as a small number of natives of Yerevan who moved there for financial reasons.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> A field assessment mission revisited the region in October 2010, confirming that there had not been much growth in population or change in the living conditions of the settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> The Co-Chairs of the Minsk Group who visited Nagorno-Karabakh, Kalbajar and Lachin in 2014 reported seeing signs of improvements in the infrastructure but could not observe any indications that the size of the population had changed in recent years.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By June 2015, an estimated 17,000 of <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a>'s once 80,000-strong Armenian population had fled the <a href="/wiki/Syrian_Civil_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Syrian Civil War">civil war</a> and sought refuge in Armenia.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/David_Babayan" title="David Babayan">David Babayan</a>, spokesperson of the Artsakh leader Bako Sahakyan, confirmed that some of those refugees had been resettled in Artsakh.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist">The Economist</a></i> put the number of the resettled families at 30 as of June 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> In December 2014, Armenian media cited local municipal authorities in stating that dozens of Syrian Armenian families had been resettled in the disputed zone, in particular in the city of <a href="/wiki/Lachin" title="Lachin">Lachin</a> and the village of <a href="/wiki/Xanl%C4%B1q" title="Xanlıq">Xanlıq</a> in <a href="/wiki/Qubadli_District" title="Qubadli District">Qubadli</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Azerbaijan's Minister of Foreign Affairs <a href="/wiki/Elmar_Mammadyarov" title="Elmar Mammadyarov">Elmar Mammadyarov</a> expressed his concern over Armenia's attempts to change the demographic situation in the region and informed of his intention to raise this issue with the Minsk Group.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2019, Armenia's <a href="/wiki/National_Security_Service_(Armenia)" title="National Security Service (Armenia)">National Security Service</a> director <a href="/wiki/Artur_Vanetsyan" title="Artur Vanetsyan">Artur Vanetsyan</a> visited Nagorno-Karabakh amid public concern about <a href="/wiki/Nikol_Pashinyan" title="Nikol Pashinyan">Nikol Pashinyan</a>'s government alleged readiness to cede some of the Armenian-controlled territories as part of a peace settlement. Vanetsyan pointed out that settling Armenians and investing into infrastructural projects along the Iranian border, in the previously Azeri-populated regions outside of the former autonomous province, was "a clear message" to the international community that there would be no territorial concessions. He referred to the ongoing settlement efforts as a method of "guaranteeing security".<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Azerbaijan's Foreign Ministry reacted by qualifying Vanetsyan's statement as an "attempt to undermine the peace talks and defy the work of the mediators" and vowed to address the issue to the UN and the OSCE.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The ceasefire ending the 2020 war stipulated that these territories were to be turned over to Azerbaijani control. Armenian settlers in these areas evacuated prior to the arrival of Azerbaijani forces.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Artsakh" title="Religion in Artsakh">Religion in Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach,_Klasztor_Gandzasar_(14).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg/220px-2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg/330px-2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg/440px-2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach%2C_Klasztor_Gandzasar_%2814%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4086" data-file-height="3044" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_G%C3%B3rski_Karabach,_Klasztor_Gandzasar_(14).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Gandzasar_monastery" title="Gandzasar monastery">Gandzasar Cathedral</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Amaras-church.jpeg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Amaras-church.jpeg/220px-Amaras-church.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="234" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Amaras-church.jpeg/330px-Amaras-church.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Amaras-church.jpeg/440px-Amaras-church.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="511" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Amaras-church.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Church of St. Grigoris of the <a href="/wiki/Amaras_Monastery" title="Amaras Monastery">Amaras Monastery</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg/220px-%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg/330px-%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg/440px-%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="531" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%94%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B8%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BA.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Dadivank_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Dadivank Monastery">Dadivank Monastery</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg/220px-Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="192" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg/330px-Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg/440px-Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1756" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghazanchetsots_Double_Rainbow.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ghazanchetsots_Cathedral" title="Ghazanchetsots Cathedral">Ghazanchetsots Cathedral</a></div></div></div>
<p>Most of the Armenian population in Artsakh is Christian and are adherents of the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church" title="Armenian Apostolic Church">Armenian Apostolic Church</a>, which is an <a href="/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" title="Oriental Orthodox">Oriental Orthodox</a> Church. Some <a href="/wiki/Orthodox_Christianity" class="mw-redirect" title="Orthodox Christianity">Eastern Orthodox</a> and <a href="/wiki/Evangelical" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical">Evangelical</a> denominations also exist.<sup id="cite_ref-Overview_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Overview-133">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Armenian_monasteries_and_churches">Armenian monasteries and churches</span></h4>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Controlled_by_Artsakh_after_2020">Controlled by Artsakh after 2020</span></h5>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Amaras_Monastery" title="Amaras Monastery">Amaras Monastery</a> (4th century), located near the villages of <a href="/wiki/Sos,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Sos, Nagorno-Karabakh">Sos</a> and <a href="/wiki/Machkalashen" title="Machkalashen">Machkalashen</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Martuni_Province" title="Martuni Province">Martuni Province</a>. According to medieval chroniclers <a href="/wiki/Pavstos_Byuzand" class="mw-redirect" title="Pavstos Byuzand">Faustus Byuzand</a> and <a href="/wiki/Movses_Kaghankatvatsi" title="Movses Kaghankatvatsi">Movses Kaghankatvatsi</a>, <a href="/wiki/St._Gregory_the_Illuminator" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Gregory the Illuminator">St. Gregory the Illuminator</a>, the patron saint and evangeliser of Armenia, founded the Amaras Monastery at the start of the fourth century.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> At the beginning of the fifth century <a href="/wiki/Mesrop_Mashtots" title="Mesrop Mashtots">Mesrop Mashtots</a>, the inventor of the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_alphabet" title="Armenian alphabet">Armenian alphabet</a>, established in Amaras the first-ever school that used his script.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Yeghishe_Arakyal_Monastery" title="Yeghishe Arakyal Monastery">Yeghishe Arakyal Monastery</a> (5th–13th centuries) commemorating St. Yeghishe, the famous evangeliser of Armenia's eastern lands. The church serves as a burial ground for the fifth century's King Vachagan II the Pious, the most well-known representative of the Arranshahik line of east Armenian monarchs. The monastery is located in the <a href="/wiki/Martakert_Province" title="Martakert Province">Martakert Province</a>.</li>
<li>Bri Yeghtsi Monastery (13th century) that centres on embedded khachkars, unique-to-Armenia stone memorials with engraved crosses. The monastery is located near the village of <a href="/wiki/Hatsi" title="Hatsi">Hatsi</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Martuni_Province" title="Martuni Province">Martuni Province</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gandzasar_monastery" title="Gandzasar monastery">Gandzasar monastery</a> (13th century), ("Գանձասար" in Armenian) is a historical monastery in Artsakh. Artsakhi government's aim is to include the Gandzasar Monastery into the directory of the UNESCO's World Heritage Sites.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Yerits_Mankants_Monastery" title="Yerits Mankants Monastery">Yerits Mankants Monastery</a> (meaning "three infants" in Armenian; 17th century) is known for hosting the seat of Artsakh's rival clergy to that of the Holy See of Gandzasar. The monastery is located in the <a href="/wiki/Martakert_Province" title="Martakert Province">Martakert Province</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Saint_John_the_Baptist_Church,_Martakert" title="Saint John the Baptist Church, Martakert">Saint John the Baptist Church</a>, located in the town of <a href="/wiki/Martakert" title="Martakert">Martakert</a>, built in 1883.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Church_of_St._Nerses_the_Great" title="Church of St. Nerses the Great">Church of St. Nerses the Great</a>, located in the town of <a href="/wiki/Martuni,_Nagorno-Karabakh" title="Martuni, Nagorno-Karabakh">Martuni</a>, consecrated in 2004. It is dedicated to the famous <a href="/wiki/Catholicos_of_Armenia" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholicos of Armenia">Armenian Catholicos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Narses" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Narses">Saint Narses</a> the Great.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Holy_Mother_of_God_Cathedral,_Stepanakert" title="Holy Mother of God Cathedral, Stepanakert">Holy Mother of God Cathedral</a> in the city of <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>, consecrated in 2019.</li></ul>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Controlled_by_Azerbaijan_after_2020">Controlled by Azerbaijan after 2020</span></h5>
<p>As a result of the <a href="/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_War" class="mw-redirect" title="2020 Nagorno-Karabakh War">2020 Nagorno-Karabakh War</a>, Azerbaijan took control over territories containing several important Armenian monasteries and churches, such as the monasteries of Dadivank, Tsitsernavank, Gtichavank, and the Ghazanchetsots Cathedral, as well as the churches of Kanach Zham and <a href="https://hy.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D5%8D%D5%B8%D6%82%D6%80%D5%A2_%D5%80%D5%B8%D5%BE%D5%B0%D5%A1%D5%B6%D5%B6%D5%A5%D5%BD_%D5%A5%D5%AF%D5%A5%D5%B2%D5%A5%D6%81%D5%AB_(%D5%8F%D5%B8%D5%B2)" class="extiw" title="hy:Սուրբ Հովհաննես եկեղեցի (Տող)">St. Hovhannes Church (Hadrut region)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Chapple_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chapple-175">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Katarovank" title="Katarovank">Katarovank Monastery</a> was founded in the fourth century, and is located close to the village of <a href="/wiki/Hin_Tagher" title="Hin Tagher">Hin Tagher</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Hadrut_Province" title="Hadrut Province">Hadrut Province</a>. The present-day chapel is a 17th-century structure. There are Armenian <a href="/wiki/Khachkars" class="mw-redirect" title="Khachkars">khachkars</a> near the chapel. The monastery offers a unique panoramic view to the <a href="/wiki/River_Araxes" class="mw-redirect" title="River Araxes">River Araxes</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tsitsernavank_Monastery" title="Tsitsernavank Monastery">Tsitsernavank Monastery</a> (4th century) is the best-preserved example of an Armenian basilica with three naves. The monastery is located in the village of <a href="/wiki/H%C3%BCs%C3%BCl%C3%BC,_Lachin" title="Hüsülü, Lachin">Tsitsernavank</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Kashatagh_Province" title="Kashatagh Province">Kashatagh Province</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dadivank" title="Dadivank">Dadivank Monastery</a> (<a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a>: <span lang="hy">Դադիվանք</span>), also known as Khutavank (<a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a>: <span lang="hy">Խութավանք</span> – Monastery on the Hill), that was built between the 9th and 13th century. It is one of the most architecturally and culturally significant monasteries in Artsakh. The western façade of Dadivank's Memorial Cathedral bears one of the most extensive Armenian lapidary (stone-inscribed) texts,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (November 2020)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Republic_of_Artsakh#The_term_seems_to_be_literally_adopted_from_a_foreign_language_and_doesn&#39;t_mean_the_same_in_English." title="Talk:Republic of Artsakh">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> and has one of the largest collection of medieval Armenian <a href="/wiki/Frescoes" class="mw-redirect" title="Frescoes">frescoes</a>. Dadivank is named after St. Dadi, a disciple of <a href="/wiki/Apostle_Thaddeus" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostle Thaddeus">Apostle Thaddeus</a> who preached the Holy Gospel in Artsakh in the first century. St. Dadi's tomb was later discovered by archaeologists in 2007. The monastery is in the <a href="/wiki/Shahumyan_Province" title="Shahumyan Province">Shahumyan Province</a> and has been placed under the protection of the Russian peacekeeping forces.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gtichavank" title="Gtichavank">Gtichavank</a> Monastery (13th century) has design features shared with the architectural style of medieval Armenia's capital city of Ani. The monastery is located in the <a href="/wiki/Hadrut_Province" title="Hadrut Province">Hadrut Province</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ghazanchetsots_Cathedral" title="Ghazanchetsots Cathedral">Ghazanchetsots Cathedral</a>, built 1868–1888 (Սուրբ Ամենափրկիչ Ղազանչեցոց Եկեղեցի – "Surb Amenap'rkich Ghazanchets'ots' Yekeghets'i" in <a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a>), also known as the <i>Cathedral of Christ the Savior</i> and the <i>Shushi Cathedral</i>, is an <a href="/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church" title="Armenian Apostolic Church">Armenian church</a> located in <a href="/wiki/Shusha" title="Shusha">Shusha</a>. It is the main cathedral and headquarters of the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church" title="Armenian Apostolic Church">Armenian Apostolic Church</a>'s "Diocese of Artsakh".</li>
<li>Just uphill from the cathedral in Shusha is the church of <a href="/wiki/Kanach_Zham" title="Kanach Zham">Kanach Zham</a> ('Green Church' in Armenian), built in 1847.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_(03).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg/220px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg/330px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg/440px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_%2803%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4656" data-file-height="3104" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Pa%C5%84stwowy_Uniwersytet_Arcachu_(03).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Artsakh_State_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Artsakh State University">Artsakh State University</a></div></div></div>
<p>Education in Artsakh is compulsory, and is free up to the age of 18. The education system is inherited from the <a href="/wiki/Education_in_the_Soviet_Union" title="Education in the Soviet Union">old system</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Artsakh's school system was severely damaged because of the 1991–1994 conflict. But the government of the Republic of Artsakh with considerable aid from the Republic of <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a> and with donations from the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_diaspora" title="Armenian diaspora">Armenian diaspora</a>, rebuilt many of the schools. Prior to the 2020 war, Artsakh had around 250 schools of various sizes, with more than 200 lying in the regions. The student population was estimated at more than 20,000, with almost half in the capital city of <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Artsakh_State_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Artsakh State University">Artsakh State University</a> was founded by Artsakh and Armenian governments' joint efforts, with main campus in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>. The university opening ceremony took place on 10 May 1992.
</p><p>Yerevan University of Management also opened a branch in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_Republic_of_Artsakh" title="Economy of the Republic of Artsakh">Economy of the Republic of Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg/220px-Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg/330px-Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg/440px-Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1160" data-file-height="870" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_downtown_2015.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A hotel in downtown Stepanakert</div></div></div>
<p>The socio-economic situation of the Republic of Artsakh was greatly affected by the 1991–1994 conflict. Yet, foreign investments began to come. The origin of most venture capital comes from Armenians in Armenia, Russia, United States, France, Australia, Iran, and the Middle East.
</p><p>Notably the telecommunications sector was developed with Karabakh Telecom<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> investing millions of dollars in mobile telephony, spearheaded by a Lebanese company.
</p><p>Copper and gold mining has been advancing since 2002 with development and launch of operations at <a href="/wiki/Drmbon" class="mw-redirect" title="Drmbon">Drmbon</a> deposit.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> Approximately 27–28 thousand tons (wet weight) of concentrates are produced<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> with average copper content of 19–21% and gold content of 32–34 g/t.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Azerbaijan considers any mining operations in Nagorno-Karabakh illegal and has vowed to engage an international audit company to determine the damages suffered by Azerbaijan's state-run ore management company as a result. In 2018, the government of Azerbaijan announced that it was planning to appeal to an international court and the law enforcement agencies of the countries where the mining companies involved are registered.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The banking system is administered by <a href="/wiki/Artsakhbank" title="Artsakhbank">Artsakhbank</a> (a Yerevan-based Armenian bank fulfilling the functions of the state bank of Nagorno-Karabakh) and a number of other Armenian banks. The republic uses the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_dram" title="Armenian dram">Armenian dram</a>.
</p><p>Wine growing and processing of agricultural products, particularly wine (i.e., storage of wine, wine stuffs, cognac alcohol) is one of the prioritised directions of the economic development.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Karmir-shuka.jpeg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Karmir-shuka.jpeg/220px-Karmir-shuka.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Karmir-shuka.jpeg/330px-Karmir-shuka.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Karmir-shuka.jpeg/440px-Karmir-shuka.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Karmir-shuka.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Karmir_Shuka" title="Karmir Shuka">Karmir Shuka</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Prior to the 2020 war, the republic developed a tourist industry geared to Armenia and the <a href="/wiki/Armenian_diaspora" title="Armenian diaspora">Armenian diaspora</a>. The republic showed a major increase in tourists over the last several years because of Artsakh's many cultural sights. Before the 2020 war there were nine<sup id="cite_ref-Solutions_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Solutions-184">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:No_original_research#Primary,_secondary_and_tertiary_sources" title="Wikipedia:No original research"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable secondary sources. (June 2022)">non-primary source needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> hotels in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>. The Artsakh development agency says 4,000 tourists visited Artsakh in 2005. The figures rose to 8,000 in 2010 (excluding visitors from Armenia).<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> The agency cooperated with the Armenia Tourism Development Agency (ATDA) as Armenia is the only way tourists (mainly Armenians) can access Artsakh. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Artsakh reported continuous expansion of visitors' geography.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> Tourist infrastructure was developed around sites such as monasteries that showcase the Armenian history in the region,<sup id="cite_ref-Toal_24-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Toal-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> with Islamic sites rarely restored,<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> while some ghost cities and areas near the front line were off limit to tourists.<sup id="cite_ref-Toal_24-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Toal-24">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Tourism Development Agency of Artsakh was established in Yerevan as a non-governmental organisation in the Republic of Armenia to promote tourism further in Artsakh. It makes preparations for tour operators, travel agencies and journalists covering the region, and arranges for hotel services, shopping, catering, recreation centers.
</p><p>Tourist attractions included:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Gandzasar_monastery" title="Gandzasar monastery">Gandzasar monastery</a>, main tourist attraction.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ghazanchetsots_Cathedral" title="Ghazanchetsots Cathedral">Ghazanchetsots Cathedral</a> of the Holy Savior (Under Azerbaijani control since 2020 war)</li>
<li>Church of the Holy Mother of God "<a href="/wiki/Kanach_Zham" title="Kanach Zham">Kanach Zham</a>" (Under Azerbaijani control since 2020 war)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Amaras_Monastery" title="Amaras Monastery">Amaras Monastery</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tsitsernavank_Monastery" title="Tsitsernavank Monastery">Tsitsernavank Monastery</a> (Under Azerbaijani control since 2020 war)</li>
<li>St. <a href="/wiki/Yeghishe_Arakyal_Monastery" title="Yeghishe Arakyal Monastery">Yeghishe Arakyal Monastery</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dadivank_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Dadivank Monastery">Dadivank Monastery</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gtichavank_monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Gtichavank monastery">Gtichavank monastery</a> (Under Azerbaijani control since 2020 war)</li>
<li>Bri Yeghtsi monastery</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Yeritsmankants_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Yeritsmankants Monastery">Yerits Mankants</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Katarovank_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Katarovank Monastery">Katarovank Monastery</a> (Under Azerbaijani control since 2020 war)</li></ul>
<p>Other tourist attractions included:
</p>
<ul><li>Fort Mayraberd (10th–18th centuries) served as the primary bulwark against Turko-nomadic incursions from the eastern steppe. The fort is found to the east of the region's capital city of <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>.</li>
<li>Govharagha Mosque (18th century), a mosque located in the city of Shusha, now under Azerbaijani control.</li></ul>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG/220px-Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG/330px-Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG/440px-Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Karintak-IMG_1445.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Section of <a href="/wiki/Janapar" title="Janapar">Janapar</a> trail.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Janapar" title="Janapar">Janapar</a> Trail is a marked trail, through mountains, valleys, and villages of Artsakh, with monasteries and fortresses along the way.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> It's not hikable since the 2020 Nagorno-Karabakh war. The trail was broken into day hikes, which brought tourists to a different village each night.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> The paths have existed for centuries but now are marked specifically for hikers. The Himnakan Janapar (backbone trail), marked in 2007, leads from the northwest region of <a href="/wiki/Shahumian" class="mw-redirect" title="Shahumian">Shahumian</a> to the southern town of <a href="/wiki/Hadrut_(town)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hadrut (town)">Hadrut</a>, now under Azerbaijani control. Side trails and mini trails take one to additional parts of Artsakh. The important sites passed along this hike include <a href="/wiki/Dadivank_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Dadivank Monastery">Dadivank Monastery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gandzasar_monastery" title="Gandzasar monastery">Gandzasar monastery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shusha" title="Shusha">Shusha</a>, the Karkar Canyon with its high cliffs, Zontik Waterfall, and the ruins of Hunot and <a href="/wiki/Gtichavank" title="Gtichavank">Gtichavank</a> monastery.
</p><p>One of the noteworthy side trails is the Gtichavank Loop Trail. This loop starts from <a href="/wiki/Tugh" class="mw-redirect" title="Tugh">Tugh</a> Village, now under Azerbaijani control.
</p><p>The cost of staying in Artsakh is relatively cheap in comparison with the rest of the region and varies approximately between 25 – US$70 for a single person as of May 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-Solutions_184-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Solutions-184">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, those who travelled to Artsakh without the Azerbaijani government's prior consent and permission will be denied entry to Azerbaijan since the country considers Artsakh their territory unlawfully occupied by the <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Armenia" title="Armed Forces of Armenia">Armenian army</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> The Azerbaijani government also keeps and publishes online a list of foreign nationals who visited these occupied areas without prior approval.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> In late 2017, the list contained 699 names with additional details (date, country, profession, purpose of visit). The earliest entry recorded a visit to Artsakh that occurred on an unspecified date sometime between 1993 and 1996. The list includes many journalists and members of parliaments of foreign countries.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Artsakh_Wine_Fest">Artsakh Wine Fest</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Wine_Fest" title="Artsakh Wine Fest">Artsakh Wine Fest</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Artcraft.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Artcraft.jpg/220px-Artcraft.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Artcraft.jpg/330px-Artcraft.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Artcraft.jpg/440px-Artcraft.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Artcraft.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Exhibition of artworks at Artsakh Wine Fest</div></div></div>
<p>Before the 2020 war, the <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Wine_Fest" title="Artsakh Wine Fest">Artsakh Wine Fest</a> took place annually in <a href="/wiki/Togh" class="mw-redirect" title="Togh">Togh</a> since 2014. The festival was held on the third Saturday of each September.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The festival was initiated by the Department of Tourism and Protection of Historical Places of the Ministry of Culture, Tourism and Youth Affairs of the Republic of Artsakh and was aimed to develop tourism in Artsakh.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> It was meant to restore Artsakh <a href="/wiki/Winemaking" title="Winemaking">winemaking</a> traditions. The festival provided a platform to the winemakers of Artsakh and Armenia giving them an opportunity to sell their products, exchange knowledge, promote their wine etc. The annual festival's program included grape stomping, tasting of traditional Artsakh cuisine, an exhibition of artworks, an exhibition of ancient artefacts that belonged to the Melik Yegan's Palace, as well as an exhibition and sale of local wine, where one could find products from 5 different regions of Artsakh and <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a>. Traditionally, the festival was accompanied by Armenian national singing and dancing. The festival evolved into a national holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG/220px-Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG/330px-Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG/440px-Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepanakert_Airport_Daytime.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Stepanakert_Airport" title="Stepanakert Airport">Stepanakert Airport</a></div></div></div>
<p>The transportation system had been damaged by the 1991–1994 conflict. The North–South Artsakh motorway alone largely facilitated the development of the transportation system.<sup id="cite_ref-Armenia_Fund_USA_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Armenia_Fund_USA-195">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Before the 2020 war, the 169-kilometre (105&#160;mi) Hadrut-Stepanakert-Askeran-Martakert motorway  was the lifeline of Artsakh, and $25 million donated during the Hayastan All-Armenian Foundation telethons was allotted for the construction of the road.<sup id="cite_ref-Armenia_Fund_USA_195-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Armenia_Fund_USA-195">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A new route from the Armenian capital <a href="/wiki/Yerevan" title="Yerevan">Yerevan</a> to Stepanakert was planned to bypass the 8–9 hours drive via the Lachin corridor.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> It was opened in September 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> A third road was planned in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Following the 2020 war, a new road will be built along the Lachin corridor to bypass Shusha.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Authorities in the USSR opened a railway line in the Nagorno-Karabakh Autonomous Oblast in 1944. It line connected the capital, Stepanakert, and <a href="/wiki/Yevlax" class="mw-redirect" title="Yevlax">Yevlax</a> in Azerbaijan. It was built to Russian standard gauge of 1520mm. Due to the first Nagorno-Karabakh War, the line the railway was badly damaged and the line was closed.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Stepanakert_Airport" title="Stepanakert Airport">Stepanakert Airport</a>, the sole civilian airport of the Republic of Artsakh, located about 8 kilometres (5 miles) east of the capital, has been closed since the onset of the war in 1990. It was expected that the airport would have regular flight services only to <a href="/wiki/Yerevan_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Yerevan Airport">Yerevan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a>, with state-owned carrier <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Air" title="Artsakh Air">Artsakh Air</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-RFE_January_27,_2011_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RFE_January_27,_2011-202">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>  As of December 2021, flights had not started.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Artsakh" title="Culture of Artsakh">Culture of Artsakh</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_(02).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg/220px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg/330px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg/440px-2014_Stepanakert%2C_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_%2802%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2692" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2014_Stepanakert,_Monument_My_i_Nasze_G%C3%B3ry_(02).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>"We are our mountains" monument depicting a man and a woman</div></div></div>
<p>"<a href="/wiki/We_Are_Our_Mountains" title="We Are Our Mountains">We Are Our Mountains</a>" (<a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a>: <span lang="hy">Մենք ենք մեր սարերը</span>) by <a href="/wiki/Sargis_Baghdasaryan" title="Sargis Baghdasaryan">Sargis Baghdasaryan</a> is a monument located in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> The sculpture is widely regarded as a symbol of the <i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i> independent Republic of Artsakh. It is a large monument from <a href="/wiki/Tuff" title="Tuff">tuff</a> of an old Armenian man and woman hewn from rock, representing the <a href="/wiki/Mountain_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Mountain people">mountain people</a> of Artsakh. It is also known as <i>Tatik yev Papik</i> (Տատիկ և Պապիկ) in Armenian. The sculpture is featured prominently on <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Nagorno-Karabakh" class="mw-redirect" title="Coat of arms of Nagorno-Karabakh">Artsakh's coat of arms</a>. Artsakh has often been portrayed as a "shield" to the Armenian nation that protects it from <a href="/wiki/Pan-Turkism" title="Pan-Turkism">Pan-Turkism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> When lecturing his fighters during the First Nagorno Karabakh-War, <a href="/wiki/Monte_Melkonian" title="Monte Melkonian">Monte Melkonian</a> would say "If we lose this land, we turn the last page on Armenian history."<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Artsakh_State_Museum_(37512504202).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg/220px-Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg/330px-Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg/440px-Artsakh_State_Museum_%2837512504202%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Artsakh_State_Museum_(37512504202).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Artsakh State Museum</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Artsakh_State_Museum" title="Artsakh State Museum">Artsakh State Museum</a> is the historical museum of the Republic of Artsakh. Located at 4 Sasunstsi David Street, in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>, the museum offers an assortment of ancient artefacts and Christian manuscripts. There are also more recent items, ranging in date from the 19th century to World War II and from events of the Karabakh Independence War.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Publications">Publications</span></h3>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/Azat_Artsakh" title="Azat Artsakh">Azat Artsakh</a></i> is the official newspaper of the Republic of Artsakh.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepenakert_view_no.2,_cropped.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg/220px-Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="119" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg/330px-Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg/440px-Stepenakert_view_no.2%2C_cropped.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2944" data-file-height="1588" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stepenakert_view_no.2,_cropped.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Stepanakert_Republican_Stadium" title="Stepanakert Republican Stadium">Stepanakert Republican Stadium</a> in <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a></div></div></div>
<p>Sports in the Republic of Artsakh are organised by the Artsakh Ministry of Culture and Youth. Due to the non-recognition of Artsakh, sports teams from the country cannot compete in most international tournaments.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the most popular sport in Artsakh. <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a> has a well-built football stadium. Since the mid-1990s, football teams from Artsakh started taking part in some domestic competitions in Armenia. <a href="/wiki/Lernayin_Artsakh_FC" title="Lernayin Artsakh FC">Lernayin Artsakh FC</a> represents the city of <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a>. In Artsakh, domestic football clubs play in the <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_Football_League" title="Artsakh Football League">Artsakh Football League</a>. The Artsakh football league was launched in 2009. The <a href="/wiki/Artsakh_national_football_team" title="Artsakh national football team">Artsakh national football team</a> was formed in 2012 and played their first competitive match against the <a href="/wiki/Abkhazia_national_football_team" title="Abkhazia national football team">Abkhazia national football team</a> in <a href="/wiki/Sokhumi" class="mw-redirect" title="Sokhumi">Sokhumi</a>, a match that ended with a result of 1–1 draw.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> The return match between the unrecognised teams took place at the <a href="/wiki/Stepanakert" title="Stepanakert">Stepanakert</a> Stadium, on 21 October 2012, when the team from Artsakh defeated the Abkhazian team 3–0.
</p><p>There is also interest in other sports, including basketball and volleyball. Sailing is practised in the town of <a href="/wiki/Martakert" title="Martakert">Martakert</a>.
</p><p>Artsakh sports teams and athletes also participate in the <a href="/wiki/Pan-Armenian_Games" title="Pan-Armenian Games">Pan-Armenian Games</a> organised in Armenia.
</p>

</li>

<li id="cite_note-37"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-37">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://armenian.usc.edu/qa-with-arsene-saparov-no-evidence-that-stalin-gave-karabakh-to-azerbaijan/">"Q&amp;A with Arsène Saparov: No Evidence that Stalin 'Gave' Karabakh to Aхerbaijan"</a>. <i>armenian.usc.edu</i>. 10 December 2018. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20201215061933/https://armenian.usc.edu/qa-with-arsene-saparov-no-evidence-that-stalin-gave-karabakh-to-azerbaijan/">Archived</a> from the original on 15 December 2020<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">27 October</span> 2022</span>. <q>Of all the documents I have seen, there is no direct evidence of Stalin doing or saying something in those 12 days in the summer of 1921 that [resulted in this decision on Karabakh]. <li id="cite_note-38"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-38">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFService2006" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Robert_Service_(historian)" title="Robert Service (historian)">Service, Robert</a> (2006). <i>Stalin: a Biography</i>. Harvard University Press. p.&#160;204. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/0-674-02258-0" title="Special:BookSources/0-674-02258-0"><bdi>0-674-02258-0</bdi></a>. <q>But on balance it was Stalin's judgement that the Azerbaijani authorities should be placated. Revolutionary pragmatism was his main motive. The Party Central Committee in Moscow gave a high priority to winning support for the Communist International across Asia. Bolshevik indulgence to 'Moslem' Azerbaijan would be noted with approval in the countries bordering the new Soviet republics. In any case, the Turkish government of Kemal Pasha was being courted by Moscow; armies of Turks had rampaged into Georgia, Armenia and Azerbaijan in recent years and continued to pose a threat to Soviet security: the appeasement of Azerbaijan was thought an effective way of keeping Istanbul quiet</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Stalin%3A+a+Biography&amp;rft.pages=204&amp;rft.pub=Harvard+University+Press&amp;rft.date=2006&amp;rft.isbn=0-674-02258-0&amp;rft.aulast=Service&amp;rft.aufirst=Robert&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ARepublic+of+Artsakh" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>

Chapter XIV. <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>After accepting the rank of [the country's] high priest, he left to enlighten the countries of Iberia and Aluank. <ul><li>* The <a href="/wiki/Political_status_of_Kosovo" title="Political status of Kosovo">political status of Kosovo</a> is disputed. Having unilaterally declared independence from <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> in 2008, Kosovo <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Kosovo" title="International recognition of Kosovo">is formally recognised as an independent state</a> by 101 out of 193 (<span data-sort-value="7001523316062176165♠" style="display:none"></span>52.3%) UN member states (with another 13 recognising it at some point but then withdrawing their recognition), while Serbia continues to claim it as part of its own territory.</li></ul>
